Executive Summary

Organizations rarely run only one kind of transformation. A single
strategic mandate can trigger a process redesign, an automation program,
a management-system certification, a culture shift, an operating-model
change, and a compliance deadline — sometimes all at once, sometimes in
sequence. This guide gives a Change Manager, Project Manager, or
Executive Sponsor one consistent way to run any of six common
transformation types on journi, without re-learning the framework each
time.

Rather than repeating a full playbook six times, this guide separates
what is genuinely common to every transformation from what is genuinely
specific to each type. Sections 2–3 cover the common ground: the same
four journi frameworks (Lewin, ADKAR, Bridges, Kübler-Ross) and the same
seven-phase lifecycle apply regardless of transformation type. Section
3's phase-by-phase tables then show, for every phase, exactly what
changes by type — so a reader can see both the shared spine and the
type-specific variation in one place. Section 5 goes deeper on each type
individually: its own natural phase names, a realistic timeline, key
tasks with RACSI ownership, and the one or two failure modes most
specific to that type.

The Six Transformation Types Covered

-   Business Process Reengineering — A radical, clean-slate redesign of
    an end-to-end business process to achieve step-change improvement in
    cost, quality, service or speed — not an incremental fix to the
    existing process.

-   Business Process Automation — Full Track — End-to-end automation of
    a defined workflow that explicitly orchestrates four kinds of
    “worker” in the same process: RPA bots for repetitive rules-based
    steps, AI/ML models for judgment-based decisioning, humans for
    exceptions and complex judgment, and system integrations connecting
    the platforms involved.

-   Integrated Management System (e.g., QHSE) — Design and certification
    of a unified management system — commonly Quality, Health, Safety
    and Environment — against one or more international standards (ISO
    9001, ISO 45001, ISO 14001, etc.), integrating policy, process and
    controls into a single auditable system.

-   Cultural / Values Transformation — A deliberate shift in an
    organization's shared values, norms and behaviors — how people
    actually act, not merely what they are told — to bring culture into
    alignment with strategy.

-   Operating Model Redesign — Redesign of how the organization is
    structured and coordinated to deliver its strategy — org structure,
    governance, decision rights, capabilities, locations and ways of
    working — typically spanning multiple functions at once.

-   Compliance-Driven Change — Change mandated by an external
    regulatory, legal or contractual requirement, implemented to bring
    the organization into — and keep it in — compliance, with control
    design and auditable evidence of compliance as first-class outputs.

How to Use This Guide

If you are running a single transformation, read Sections 1–3 for the
shared foundation, then go directly to your type's subsection in Section
5 for the operational detail. If you are running several transformation
types concurrently — a common real-world situation — Section 3's
phase-by-phase comparison and Section 6's cross-type matrix are built
specifically to help you see where the programs should stay coordinated
(for example, sharing one Steering Committee and one Composite Readiness
Index view) and where they genuinely need different governance.

1. Introduction

1.1 Purpose of This Guide

This guide is a practitioner's companion to journi for six of the most
common transformation types an organization runs: Business Process
Reengineering, Business Process Automation (Full Track), an Integrated
Management System such as QHSE, Cultural / Values Transformation,
Operating Model Redesign, and Compliance-Driven Change. It assumes the
reader already understands journi's four underlying frameworks at a
conceptual level, or has read the companion ERP Implementation guide,
which covers them in full depth.

1.2 Who This Guide Is For

-   Change Manager (CM) — owns the four framework readings and the
    adoption plan for whichever transformation type is in flight.

-   Program / Project Manager (PM) — owns scope, schedule, budget and
    delivery milestones specific to the transformation type.

-   Executive Sponsor (ES) — owns the business case and clears
    organizational barriers, and in several types (IMS, Compliance)
    carries specific external-facing accountability.

-   Frontline Supervisors (SUP) — coach the cohort day to day through
    whichever validation and deployment phase applies.

1.3 Why One Guide, Not Six

Every transformation type in this guide, examined closely, follows the
same seven-phase spine: establish the case and diagnose the current
state, design the target state, build it, validate it, deploy it,
stabilize it, and sustain it. What differs is not the spine but what
happens inside each phase — a “mock audit” in an IMS program and a
“pilot” in a Business Process Reengineering program are doing the same
job (validate before full deployment) in different language, with
different artifacts and a different external party involved. Treating
these as six unrelated methodologies obscures that shared structure and
makes it harder to run two transformation types together, which is why
this guide is organized around the common lifecycle first, and the
type-specific variation second.

2. The Common Foundation — journi's Four Frameworks

Every transformation type in this guide is read through the same four
frameworks, at the same four altitudes, regardless of what is actually
being transformed. This is deliberate: a process, a workflow, a
management system, a culture, an org structure, and a set of controls
are all, from the standpoint of the people living through them, a change
to be unfrozen, adopted, mourned or embraced, and felt — in that order,
on four different clocks. The condensed primer below applies without
modification to all six types; the companion ERP Implementation guide
contains the full signal catalogue at greater length.

2.1 Lewin's Unfreeze – Change – Refreeze

Kurt Lewin, 1947 · Altitude: organizational / systemic.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Stage      Meaning               Signs It Is Active
  ---------- --------------------- ---------------------------------------
  Unfreeze   Building the case for Leadership publicly questions “the way
             change and loosening  we've always done it”; hallway/chat
             attachment to the     chatter rises; people ask “why now?”;
             status quo.           requests for job-security reassurance
                                   increase; early skepticism (“we tried
                                   this before”).

  Change     The transition itself Old-process usage falls and new-process
             — old patterns        usage rises in system data; help-desk
             released, new ones    tickets spike; workarounds appear;
             not yet habitual.     supervisors coach more on the floor; a
                                   temporary productivity dip; informal
                                   peer champions emerge.

  Refreeze   The new state is      The new process is cited without
             deliberately          qualification; onboarding materials
             stabilized until it   updated; old system access retired;
             is simply “how things KPIs stable across multiple cycles; the
             are done.”            change survives a manager turnover.
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------

Signal catalogue — 2.1 Lewin's Unfreeze – Change – Refreeze.

2.3 Bridges' Transition Model

William Bridges, Managing Transitions, 1991 · Altitude: psychological /
identity.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Stage       Meaning                Signs to Look For
  ----------- ---------------------- ------------------------------------
  Ending      Letting go of the old  Spontaneous expressions of loss;
              identity, competence   “old days” storytelling; resistance
              and relationships —    to retiring old tools; denial
              often with real loss.  statements; a dip in discretionary
                                     effort.

  Neutral     The disorienting       Confusion about roles and “which
  Zone        in-between — old ways  rule applies now”; a temporary
              gone, new ways not yet morale dip; ambiguity-driven errors;
              automatic.             active rumor mill; requests for more
                                     structure.

  New         Genuine buy-in and a   New vocabulary (“we're a digital
  Beginning   new identity forming — team now”); visible pride; teaching
              ownership, not         newcomers unprompted;
              compliance.            forward-looking improvement
                                     suggestions.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Signal catalogue — 2.3 Bridges' Transition Model.

2.4 Kübler-Ross Change Curve

Adapted from Elisabeth Kübler-Ross, 1969 · Altitude: emotional /
moment-to-moment.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Stage         Meaning               Signs to Look For
  ------------- --------------------- --------------------------------------
  Denial        The change is not yet “This doesn't really apply to us”;
                accepted as real or   unopened comms; business-as-usual
                personally relevant.  behavior; minimal engagement at info
                                      sessions.

  Resistance /  Active, visible       Vocal public complaints;
  Anger         pushback — a normal,  blame-shifting; escalations to
                necessary phase, not  leadership; sarcasm; slow-walking
                pure negativity.      tasks; rising interpersonal friction.

  Exploration   Cautious engagement   Tentative questions rather than flat
                mixing curiosity with refusal; willingness to pilot; mixed
                residual doubt.       sentiment in the same room; sandbox
                                      experimentation.

  Commitment    Active, willing       Proactive unprompted use in real work;
                participation,        peer advocacy; improvement suggestions
                sustained without     rather than requests to revert.
                being watched.        
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------

Signal catalogue — 2.4 Kübler-Ross Change Curve.

2.5 How the Balance Shifts by Type

While all four frameworks apply to every type, their relative weight in
practice shifts. Cultural / Values Transformation leans heavily on
Bridges and Kübler-Ross, since the change is identity-based rather than
procedural. Compliance-Driven Change leans heavily on ADKAR's Awareness
and Knowledge blocks, since the rules must be followed exactly and
Desire is often assumed rather than cultivated. Business Process
Automation and Business Process Reengineering lean on Ability and
Reinforcement, since the transformation succeeds or fails on whether
people (and, for Automation, systems) can actually perform the new work
under real conditions. Section 5 notes this balance explicitly for each
type.

3. The Common Transformation Lifecycle

Every transformation type in this guide follows the same seven phases
below. Each phase entry gives the generic definition, the tasks and
RACSI ownership common to every type, and then — for every one of the
six types — what is specific about that phase for that type. This is the
single most useful section in the guide for anyone running more than one
transformation type at once: read down a phase to see what stays
constant, and across the by-type table to see exactly where it diverges.

Role codes: ES = Executive Sponsor, CM = Change Manager, PM =
Program/Project Manager, FPO = Functional Process Owner, ITL =
IT/Technical Lead, SUP = Frontline Supervisor, EU = End User cohort.

3.1 P1 — Intake & Diagnosis

Establish the case for change: assess the current state, quantify the
gap or risk, and confirm sponsorship and scope before any redesign work
begins.

Common Tasks (Every Type)

-   Confirm Executive Sponsor and scope

-   Assess the current state (process, system, culture, structure, or
    control, depending on type)

-   Quantify the gap, risk, or cost of inaction

-   Open the Change Management project and set the Lewin phase

-   Run the baseline ADKAR / Bridges / Kübler-Ross pulse

Common RACSI for this phase, before type-specific adjustment (see
Section 5 for type-specific RACSI).

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R (Responsible — may  A (Accountable C (Consulted) S (Support) I (Informed)
  be multiple)          — exactly one)                           
  --------------------- -------------- ------------- ----------- ------------
  PM, FPO               ES             CM            ITL         SUP, EU

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

What Is Specific to Each Type in This Phase

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Transformation Type What's Specific in This Phase
  ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Business Process    Current-state diagnosis is deliberately unconstrained
  Reengineering       by the existing process's own logic — the team maps
                      what happens today but explicitly brackets “why it's
                      done this way” so the redesign in P2 is not anchored to
                      it.

  Business Process    Diagnosis is a process-mining and
  Automation — Full   automation-opportunity assessment: which steps are
  Track               rules-based (RPA candidates), which require judgment
                      (AI/human candidates), and where system integrations
                      already exist or must be built.

  Integrated          This phase is literally named “Intake” in the standard
  Management System   IMS lifecycle: intake of the certification requirement,
  (e.g., QHSE)        followed by a formal gap assessment against the target
                      standard(s) (e.g., ISO 9001, 45001, 14001).

  Cultural / Values   Diagnosis combines an employee survey with direct
  Transformation      behavioral observation, since the gap between stated
                      and lived values is rarely visible from survey data
                      alone.

  Operating Model     Diagnosis assesses the current operating model across
  Redesign            structure, governance, decision rights, capabilities
                      and locations — not just one process — typically via
                      structured interviews across every affected function.

  Compliance-Driven   Diagnosis is a regulatory requirement and gap analysis
  Change              against the specific legal or contractual text, usually
                      run by or with Legal/Compliance, producing a
                      control-by-control gap list rather than a process
                      pain-point list.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

3.2 P2 — Case for Change & Target-State Design

Define what the future state looks like and agree the design principles
that will govern every later build decision.

Common Tasks (Every Type)

-   Define target-state scope and design principles

-   Design the future state at a working level of detail

-   Validate the design with functional owners

-   Launch phase-appropriate communications

-   Recruit and brief the change champion network

Common RACSI for this phase, before type-specific adjustment (see
Section 5 for type-specific RACSI).

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R (Responsible — may  A (Accountable C (Consulted) S (Support) I (Informed)
  be multiple)          — exactly one)                           
  --------------------- -------------- ------------- ----------- ------------
  PM, FPO               ES             CM            ITL         SUP, EU

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

What Is Specific to Each Type in This Phase

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Transformation Type What's Specific in This Phase
  ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Business Process    Design principles are explicitly framed as “what would
  Reengineering       we do if we started from zero,” and the future-state
                      process map is allowed to cross functional and system
                      boundaries freely.

  Business Process    Target-state design is an automation architecture, not
  Automation — Full   just a process map: it specifies which steps are RPA,
  Track               which are AI-assisted, which stay human, and the
                      integration contracts between every system touched.

  Integrated          Design produces the management-system documentation
  Management System   structure itself — the manual, procedures and records
  (e.g., QHSE)        the standard requires — mapped explicitly against the
                      standard's clause numbering.

  Cultural / Values   Design defines target values and observable behaviors
  Transformation      (not aspirational language alone), plus the
                      leadership-modeling plan that will demonstrate them
                      first, before any wider communication goes out.

  Operating Model     Design produces the Target Operating Model (TOM)
  Redesign            blueprint: structure, governance, decision rights and
                      capability requirements, typically as a small number of
                      credible options before one is selected.

  Compliance-Driven   Design is control design: translating each gap
  Change              identified in P1 into a specific policy, process
                      control, or system control, mapped one-to-one back to
                      the regulatory clause it satisfies.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

3.3 P3 — Build & Development

Construct the target state: build, configure, draft, or develop whatever
the transformation type requires, validated incrementally rather than in
one long unreviewed phase.

Common Tasks (Every Type)

-   Build or develop the target-state deliverable iteratively

-   Run design-review checkpoints with functional owners

-   Maintain a versioned decision log

-   Re-run the ADKAR pulse (Awareness / early Desire)

-   Track build progress against the transition plan

Common RACSI for this phase, before type-specific adjustment (see
Section 5 for type-specific RACSI).

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R (Responsible — may  A (Accountable C (Consulted) S (Support) I (Informed)
  be multiple)          — exactly one)                           
  --------------------- -------------- ------------- ----------- ------------
  ITL, FPO              PM             CM            SUP         ES, EU

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

What Is Specific to Each Type in This Phase

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Transformation Type What's Specific in This Phase
  ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Business Process    Build includes both the new process itself and the
  Reengineering       organizational changes it implies — revised roles,
                      reporting lines, or handoffs — built and reviewed
                      together rather than the org change being deferred to a
                      later phase.

  Business Process    Build runs three parallel tracks — RPA bot development,
  Automation — Full   AI model integration or fine-tuning, and
  Track               system-integration builds — each with its own technical
                      review, converging into one orchestrated workflow.

  Integrated          Build is the rollout of new or revised procedures,
  Management System   records and controls into daily operations, together
  (e.g., QHSE)        with the internal audit program that will later test
                      them.

  Cultural / Values   Build is the reinforcement infrastructure: recognition
  Transformation      mechanisms, symbolic actions, and system changes (e.g.,
                      how performance is reviewed) that make the target
                      behaviors visible and rewarded, not a document.

  Operating Model     Build is the detailed org design — role descriptions,
  Redesign            decision-rights matrices, governance forums — plus the
                      transition plan sequencing how the organization moves
                      from current to target structure.

  Compliance-Driven   Build is control implementation and mandatory training:
  Change              configuring the actual control (system setting, process
                      step, policy) and delivering the training and
                      attestation the regulation requires as evidence.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

3.4 P4 — Validation

Test the built target state under realistic conditions before wider
deployment — named differently by type (pilot, mock audit, UAT) but
serving the same purpose: catch problems while they are still cheap to
fix.

Common Tasks (Every Type)

-   Run the validation exercise (pilot / mock audit / UAT, per type)

-   Log and triage defects or findings

-   Cross-check Knowledge/Ability against Bridges (Divergence Pattern
    check)

-   Remediate findings before sign-off

-   Obtain formal sign-off to proceed

Common RACSI for this phase, before type-specific adjustment (see
Section 5 for type-specific RACSI).

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R (Responsible — may  A (Accountable C (Consulted) S (Support) I (Informed)
  be multiple)          — exactly one)                           
  --------------------- -------------- ------------- ----------- ------------
  FPO, ITL              PM             CM            SUP         ES, EU

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

What Is Specific to Each Type in This Phase

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Transformation Type What's Specific in This Phase
  ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Business Process    Validation is a pilot of the new process in one site,
  Reengineering       team, or region, run in parallel with (or as a
                      controlled replacement of) the old process, with hard
                      before/after performance metrics compared.

  Business Process    Validation is UAT plus a hypercare-style shadow period:
  Automation — Full   the automated workflow runs alongside the manual one,
  Track               with every AI/bot decision reviewed by a human until
                      accuracy and exception rates are proven.

  Integrated          This phase is the Mock-up Audit — an internal or
  Management System   third-party pre-assessment run exactly like the real
  (e.g., QHSE)        certification audit, deliberately designed to surface
                      nonconformities before the certifying body ever sees
                      them.

  Cultural / Values   Validation is a pilot cohort or business unit where the
  Transformation      target behaviors are modeled and reinforced first,
                      checked via the same survey and observation methods
                      used in diagnosis to confirm real behavioral movement,
                      not just awareness.

  Operating Model     Validation is a pilot transition of one function or
  Redesign            region into the target structure, testing the new
                      governance forums and decision rights in practice
                      before the full organization moves.

  Compliance-Driven   Validation is internal audit or independent testing of
  Change              the implemented controls against the regulatory
                      requirement, producing formal test evidence rather than
                      a pilot-performance comparison.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

3.5 P5 — Deployment

Move the target state into full production or force — rollout, go-live,
or certification, depending on type — the single discrete milestone the
rest of the program has been building toward.

Common Tasks (Every Type)

-   Execute final preparation and freeze/cutover activities

-   Execute the deployment event

-   Communicate confirmation to all cohorts

-   Activate elevated support

-   Confirm the milestone was achieved

Common RACSI for this phase, before type-specific adjustment (see
Section 5 for type-specific RACSI).

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R (Responsible — may  A (Accountable C (Consulted) S (Support) I (Informed)
  be multiple)          — exactly one)                           
  --------------------- -------------- ------------- ----------- ------------
  ITL, PM               ES             CM            SUP         FPO, EU

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

What Is Specific to Each Type in This Phase

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Transformation Type What's Specific in This Phase
  ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Business Process    Deployment is the full-scale rollout of the new process
  Reengineering       across every remaining site or team, following the
                      sequence set by the pilot's results, with the old
                      process formally retired at a defined cut-over point.

  Business Process    Deployment is production go-live for the automated
  Automation — Full   workflow at full volume, with human-in-the-loop review
  Track               levels stepped down according to a pre-agreed accuracy
                      threshold rather than all at once.

  Integrated          This phase is the Certifying Audit itself — conducted
  Management System   by an accredited external certification body, whose
  (e.g., QHSE)        pass/fail (or “pass with minor nonconformities”)
                      decision is outside the organization's control.

  Cultural / Values   Deployment is the org-wide rollout of the reinforcement
  Transformation      plan validated in the pilot — leadership modeling,
                      recognition, and systems changes — extended from the
                      pilot cohort to the full population.

  Operating Model     Deployment is the full organizational transition —
  Redesign            people moving into new roles and reporting lines, new
                      governance forums convening for the first time —
                      typically the highest-disruption single event in this
                      lifecycle.

  Compliance-Driven   Deployment is the controls going live and becoming
  Change              legally or contractually enforceable, often on a fixed
                      external date the organization does not control.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

3.6 P6 — Stabilization & Hypercare

Absorb the post-deployment adjustment period with elevated support,
close defects fast, and track whether the emotional and behavioral layer
is catching up to the technical or structural milestone.

Common Tasks (Every Type)

-   Staff elevated support for the highest-risk weeks

-   Track adoption or compliance metrics daily and triage issues by
    severity

-   Re-pulse Bridges / Kübler-Ross at defined intervals post-deployment

-   Coach any cohort showing regression

-   Taper support toward standard levels as metrics stabilize

Common RACSI for this phase, before type-specific adjustment (see
Section 5 for type-specific RACSI).

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R (Responsible — may  A (Accountable C (Consulted) S (Support) I (Informed)
  be multiple)          — exactly one)                           
  --------------------- -------------- ------------- ----------- ------------
  CM, SUP               CM             ITL, FPO      PM          ES, EU

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

What Is Specific to Each Type in This Phase

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Transformation Type What's Specific in This Phase
  ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Business Process    Stabilization tracks whether the new process's promised
  Reengineering       performance gains (cost, cycle time, quality) are
                      actually materializing at scale, not just whether
                      people are technically using the new steps.

  Business Process    Stabilization is exception-handling tuning: the
  Automation — Full   human-in-the-loop review rate is adjusted based on real
  Track               AI/bot accuracy data, and a Center of Excellence begins
                      governing bot and model changes going forward.

  Integrated          Stabilization is the period between the Certifying
  Management System   Audit and the first surveillance audit, where any minor
  (e.g., QHSE)        nonconformities raised during certification are
                      formally closed out with evidence.

  Cultural / Values   Stabilization is the longest and most fragile phase for
  Transformation      this type: early enthusiasm from the pilot needs to
                      survive contact with the full organization's skeptics
                      without the reinforcement mechanisms visibly weakening.

  Operating Model     Stabilization tracks whether decisions are actually
  Redesign            being made at the new decision rights, not silently
                      routed back to the old reporting lines out of habit.

  Compliance-Driven   Stabilization is the first live compliance-monitoring
  Change              cycle — confirming controls operate as designed under
                      real conditions, not just as tested in P4.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

3.7 P7 — Sustainment & Closure

Confirm the change has become the durable new normal, embed
reinforcement into standard business-as-usual mechanisms, and formally
close the change project.

Common Tasks (Every Type)

-   Embed new metrics into standard performance management

-   Confirm reinforcement mechanisms are active

-   Run checkpoint reviews against benchmarking bands

-   Call Refreeze formally once checkpoint evidence supports it

-   Close the project and hand off ongoing ownership

Common RACSI for this phase, before type-specific adjustment (see
Section 5 for type-specific RACSI).

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R (Responsible — may  A (Accountable C (Consulted) S (Support) I (Informed)
  be multiple)          — exactly one)                           
  --------------------- -------------- ------------- ----------- ------------
  CM                    ES             PM, FPO       SUP         ITL, EU

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

What Is Specific to Each Type in This Phase

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Transformation Type What's Specific in This Phase
  ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Business Process    Closure confirms the new process's performance metrics
  Reengineering       have held for multiple consecutive reporting cycles
                      before the transformation is declared complete, not
                      just that the old process was decommissioned.

  Business Process    Closure hands ongoing bot/AI/integration ownership to a
  Automation — Full   permanent automation Center of Excellence, since these
  Track               assets require continuous maintenance unlike a one-time
                      process change.

  Integrated          Closure is ongoing: the management system now enters a
  Management System   standing cycle of internal audits and periodic
  (e.g., QHSE)        (commonly annual) surveillance audits by the
                      certification body — the system is never really
                      “closed,” only handed to business-as-usual ownership.

  Cultural / Values   Closure is deliberately provisional: culture work is
  Transformation      formally folded into standing leadership and HR
                      practices rather than declared finished, since
                      regression risk remains high for years after the formal
                      program ends.

  Operating Model     Closure confirms the new structure has survived at
  Redesign            least one full planning or budget cycle without
                      reverting, and hands ongoing governance to the standing
                      operating rhythm.

  Compliance-Driven   Closure hands the compliance-monitoring plan to the
  Change              permanent control owner (often Legal, Compliance, or
                      Risk), with the monitoring cadence continuing
                      indefinitely as long as the regulation remains in
                      force.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

4. The Six Transformation Types — Profiles

Each profile below gives a fast, executive-level orientation to one
transformation type: what it is, when to reach for it, what typically
triggers it, how long it realistically takes, its primary artifacts, and
what makes it genuinely different from the other five.

4.1 Business Process Reengineering

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element          Detail
  ---------------- --------------------------------------------------------
  Definition       A radical, clean-slate redesign of an end-to-end
                   business process to achieve step-change improvement in
                   cost, quality, service or speed — not an incremental fix
                   to the existing process.

  When to Use      The process is fundamentally broken, incremental
                   improvement has already been tried and failed, or
                   competitive or cost pressure demands a step-change in
                   performance rather than a marginal gain.

  Typical Trigger  Persistent performance gap versus competitors; M&A
                   process integration; a major cost-reduction mandate.

  Typical Duration 6–12 months per major process.

  Primary          Current-state process map and root-cause diagnosis;
  Artifacts        clean-slate future-state design; revised org/role model;
                   transition plan.

  Key              The process itself is redesigned from a blank page
  Differentiator   rather than improved incrementally, and the redesign
                   frequently forces a downstream org or role change rather
                   than the reverse.
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

4.2 Business Process Automation — Full Track

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element          Detail
  ---------------- --------------------------------------------------------
  Definition       End-to-end automation of a defined workflow that
                   explicitly orchestrates four kinds of “worker” in the
                   same process: RPA bots for repetitive rules-based steps,
                   AI/ML models for judgment-based decisioning, humans for
                   exceptions and complex judgment, and system integrations
                   connecting the platforms involved.

  When to Use      A well-understood, high-volume, rules-heavy workflow
                   with digital inputs is ready for automation and the
                   organization wants to scale throughput without
                   proportional headcount growth.

  Typical Trigger  Cost-to-serve pressure; error-rate reduction targets; a
                   scaling mandate without headcount growth.

  Typical Duration 3–9 months per workflow, depending on the number of
                   systems and AI components involved.

  Primary          Process-mining / automation-opportunity assessment;
  Artifacts        automation architecture (bot inventory, AI model specs,
                   integration map); a RACI across bots, AI, and human
                   steps; hypercare monitoring dashboard.

  Key              Bots, AI models, and humans are treated as first-class
  Differentiator   actors in one orchestrated workflow, not as a single
                   tool bolted onto otherwise-manual work — which is why
                   RACSI ownership in this guide explicitly names which
                   actor performs each step.
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

4.3 Integrated Management System (e.g., QHSE)

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element          Detail
  ---------------- --------------------------------------------------------
  Definition       Design and certification of a unified management system
                   — commonly Quality, Health, Safety and Environment —
                   against one or more international standards (ISO 9001,
                   ISO 45001, ISO 14001, etc.), integrating policy, process
                   and controls into a single auditable system.

  When to Use      A customer or regulator requires certification,
                   previously siloed Q/H/S/E systems need consolidating, or
                   the organization is preparing for an initial or renewal
                   certification audit.

  Typical Trigger  Customer contractual requirement; regulatory mandate; a
                   strategic risk-reduction initiative.

  Typical Duration 6–14 months to first certification.

  Primary          Gap assessment against the target standard(s);
  Artifacts        management-system documentation (manual, procedures,
                   records); internal audit program; mock/pre-assessment
                   audit report; certification audit outcome.

  Key              The lifecycle is explicit and externally anchored —
  Differentiator   Intake, Diagnosis, Design, Implementation, Mock-up
                   Audit, Certifying Audit — and the final gate is a
                   pass/fail decision made by an external certification
                   body, not an internal go/no-go call.
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

4.4 Cultural / Values Transformation

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element          Detail
  ---------------- --------------------------------------------------------
  Definition       A deliberate shift in an organization's shared values,
                   norms and behaviors — how people actually act, not
                   merely what they are told — to bring culture into
                   alignment with strategy.

  When to Use      The strategy requires behaviors the current culture does
                   not support (for example, moving from risk-averse to
                   innovative), following a merger that must integrate two
                   cultures, or in response to a trust or ethics event.

  Typical Trigger  A new strategic direction; M&A culture integration; a
                   reputational or ethics event; a leadership change.

  Typical Duration 18–36 months to embed durably — culture moves more
                   slowly than any other transformation type in this guide.

  Primary          Culture diagnostic (survey plus behavioral observation);
  Artifacts        target values and behavior definitions; a
                   leadership-modeling plan; a symbolic and systemic
                   reinforcement plan (recognition, rituals, systems
                   alignment).

  Key              The object of change is behavioral and identity-based
  Differentiator   rather than procedural, so Bridges and Kübler-Ross carry
                   proportionally more analytical weight than ADKAR's
                   Knowledge/Ability blocks, and the realistic timeline is
                   structurally longer than any other type here.
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

4.5 Operating Model Redesign

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element          Detail
  ---------------- --------------------------------------------------------
  Definition       Redesign of how the organization is structured and
                   coordinated to deliver its strategy — org structure,
                   governance, decision rights, capabilities, locations and
                   ways of working — typically spanning multiple functions
                   at once.

  When to Use      Strategy or the market has outgrown the current
                   structure, cross-functional friction from unclear
                   accountabilities is persistent, or a merger or regional
                   rebalancing requires a new structure.

  Typical Trigger  A new corporate strategy; M&A integration; a significant
                   cost or agility mandate; repeated cross-functional
                   friction.

  Typical Duration 9–18 months from diagnosis to full transition.

  Primary          Current operating model assessment; target operating
  Artifacts        model (TOM) blueprint; org design and an org-level
                   decision-rights matrix; a transition / migration plan.

  Key              The unit being redesigned is the organization's
  Differentiator   structure and governance itself, not a single process —
                   and a Business Process Reengineering, Automation, or IMS
                   initiative is frequently a downstream consequence of an
                   operating-model decision rather than the reverse.
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

4.6 Compliance-Driven Change

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element          Detail
  ---------------- --------------------------------------------------------
  Definition       Change mandated by an external regulatory, legal or
                   contractual requirement, implemented to bring the
                   organization into — and keep it in — compliance, with
                   control design and auditable evidence of compliance as
                   first-class outputs.

  When to Use      A new law, regulation, or contractual clause requires
                   organizational, process or system change by a fixed
                   external deadline.

  Typical Trigger  New legislation; a regulator finding or consent order; a
                   contractual clause; an industry-standard update.

  Typical Duration Driven by the external deadline; commonly 3–12 months.

  Primary          Regulatory requirement / gap analysis; control design
  Artifacts        and policy updates; training and attestation records;
                   audit or testing evidence; an ongoing
                   compliance-monitoring plan.

  Key              An externally fixed deadline carries real legal,
  Differentiator   financial or licensing consequences for missing it,
                   which shifts the change-management emphasis toward
                   Awareness and mandatory Knowledge — rules must be
                   followed exactly — with Desire often assumed or required
                   rather than cultivated.
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

5. Type-Specific Deep Dives — Full Phase, Task, Step & RACSI Detail

Each subsection below takes one transformation type down to full
operational detail: every one of its seven phases, each phase's SIPOC
(with the Process column listing that phase's five Tasks in sequence),
every Task tagged [PROJECT] / [CHANGE] / [JOINT] with RACSI, and every
Task's Step with its own mini-SIPOC, a named technique, the technique's
goal, a detailed description, execution details, and a recommended
open-source tool. journi module cross-references appear wherever a Task
or Step touches a specific module.

Role codes: ES = Executive Sponsor, CM = Change Manager, PM =
Program/Project Manager, FPO = Functional Process Owner, ITL =
IT/Technical Lead, SUP = Frontline Supervisor, EU = End User cohort.

5.1 Business Process Reengineering

Phase Sequence (Mapped to the Common Lifecycle)

-   Diagnosis (current-state map & root-cause analysis) — maps to P1

-   Clean-Slate Redesign (future-state process & design principles) —
    maps to P2

-   Build (new process, roles, systems) — maps to P3

-   Pilot (one site or team) — maps to P4

-   Rollout (full-scale deployment) — maps to P5

-   Stabilization (performance-gain tracking) — maps to P6

-   Sustainment (locked-in new normal) — maps to P7

Typical Timeline

6–12 months for a single major process; longer for a portfolio of
processes run sequentially.

5.1.1 P1 — Diagnosis — Current-State Map & Root-Cause Analysis

Typically the first 6–8 weeks of a 6–12 month program.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Map the current-state process end to end

Step 1 — Map the process as it actually runs

The map has to capture the process as it is actually run, workarounds
included, not the process as the official procedure manual describes it
— the gap between the two is often where the real diagnosis begins.

SIPOC for this step.

  Functional     existing        workshop          workaround     Redesign team;

  frontline      (if any);                                        
  staff who run  performance                                      
  the process    data from                                        
  today.         source systems.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Facilitated process-mapping workshop

  Technique Goal  Capture the process exactly as it is performed today,
                  including informal workarounds.

  Technique       Run a facilitated workshop per process segment with the
  Details         people who perform it daily, walking every step,
                  decision point, and handoff onto a shared board.

  Recommended     BPMN.io
  Open-Source

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Diagnose root causes of underperformance

Step 1 — Diagnose the root causes

Redesigning around a symptom instead of its root cause is the single
most common way a reengineering effort quietly reproduces the old
process's problems in new clothing.

SIPOC for this step.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

  frontline      (if any);                                     
  staff who run  performance                                   
  the process    data from                                     
  today.         source systems.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Root-cause analysis (5 Whys / fishbone)

  Technique Goal  Trace every major pain point back to its structural
                  cause, not its symptom.

  Technique       For each major pain point on the current-state map, run
  Details         a structured root-cause session with the process owner
                  and 2–3 frontline staff.

  Recommended     draw.io (diagrams.net)
  Open-Source

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Design the future-state process from a blank page

Step 1 — Design the process from zero

The discipline of the exercise is what matters: the team is explicitly
asked “if we were building this today, from nothing, what would we do,”
and only afterward checks the result against real constraints.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------- -------------- --------------

  Sponsor;       root-cause     workshop                      Program
  Functional     diagnosis;                                   Manager;
  Process        quantified                                   Change
  Owners.        performance                                  Manager.
                 gap.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Clean-slate design workshop

  Technique Goal  Design the target process as if the current process and
                  its constraints did not exist.

  Technique       Facilitate a multi-day design session with the redesign
  Details         team, explicitly deferring feasibility objections to a
                  later validation pass.

  Recommended     BPMN.io
  Open-Source

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Agree design principles that break from legacy logic

Step 1 — Agree the design principles

Naming what the old process did wrong, explicitly, in the principle
itself, is what keeps the redesign from sliding back toward familiar
territory under time pressure.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process             Outputs        Customers
  -------------- -------------- ------------------- -------------- --------------
  Redesign team; As-is process  Design-principles   Signed-off     Steering

                 gap.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Design-principles workshop

  Technique Goal  Fix a small number of non-negotiable rules the
                  future-state design must satisfy.

  Technique       Facilitate a session to agree 5–8 written design
  Details         principles that explicitly reject specific elements of
                  the old process's logic (e.g. “no sequential approvals
                  — parallel only”).

  Recommended     draw.io (diagrams.net)

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Define the target org / role model implied by the new
process

Step 1 — Define the target roles

A reengineered process almost always implies a different set of roles
than the old one; naming that explicitly now, rather than discovering it
during Build, avoids a late and disruptive org surprise.

Task 4 — [CHANGE] Launch redesign-phase communications

Step 1 — Communicate the redesign direction

Role change is one of the most anxiety-triggering signals in any
transformation; a deliberate, honest communication here does more to
protect Desire than any later reassurance can recover.

Task 5 — [CHANGE] Log barrier reasons from role-change anxiety

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 6.

Step 1 — Log the barrier reasons

Role-change anxiety is rarely generic — it is almost always about a
specific fear (redeployment, reporting to a former peer, loss of title);
the barrier reason has to capture that specificity to be actionable.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Barrier-reason logging

  Technique Goal  Capture the specific, named reason behind any Desire ≤
                  2 score tied to role change.

  Technique       For every cohort scoring Desire at 2 or below, require
  Details         a one-sentence, named barrier reason before the score
                  is accepted.

Task 1 — [JOINT] Select and brief the pilot site or team

Step 1 — Select a representative pilot site

A pilot run by an unusually strong team validates a design that may not
survive contact with an average-performing site; the selection criteria
should say so explicitly.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process              Outputs        Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------------- -------------- --------------

  Process Owner; systems;                            rationale      (next phase);

  Lead.          changes; pilot                                     Manager.
                 site selection                                     
                 criteria.                                          

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Representativeness screening

  Technique Goal  Choose a pilot site that reflects average, not
                  best-case, performance conditions.

  Technique       Score candidate sites against representativeness
  Details         criteria (team tenure, current performance level,
                  complexity of caseload) before selecting the pilot
                  site.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc

  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Controlled pilot run

  Technique Goal  Operate the new process at the pilot site under real
                  conditions for a defined period.

  Technique       Run the new process at the pilot site for a defined
  Details         period (commonly 4–6 weeks), with the old process
                  available as fallback only for defined exception cases.

Task 5 — [PROJECT] Remediate pilot findings before rollout sign-off

Step 1 — Remediate the pilot findings

A pilot that surfaces zero findings is more likely to indicate an
unrealistic pilot than a flawless design; the point of this task is to
take the findings seriously and close them, not to treat their absence
as success.

SIPOC for this step.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process        Outputs          Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------- ---------------- --------------

  Functional     workflow and   sprint         pilot-findings   rollout team
  Process Owner; systems;                      log              (next phase);
  IT / Technical finalized role                                 Change
  Lead.          changes; pilot                                 Manager.
                 site selection                                 
                 criteria.                                      

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Findings remediation sprint

  Technique Goal  Close or formally accept every pilot finding before
                  recommending rollout.

  Technique       Triage every pilot finding, closing blockers and
  Details         majors, and formally accepting any minor findings with
                  FPO sign-off before the rollout go/no-go.

  Recommended     Mantis BT
  Open-Source

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Sequence the full-scale rollout plan

Step 1 — Sequence the rollout

Sequencing by readiness rather than convenience means the strongest
remaining sites go early, building momentum and proof points for the
sites that follow.

  authority).    plan.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Rollout-wave planning

  Technique Goal  Order the remaining sites into rollout waves based on
                  pilot learnings and site readiness.

  Technique       Group remaining sites into 2–4 rollout waves,
  Details         prioritizing by readiness signals (ADKAR scores,
                  complexity, resourcing) rather than a simple geographic
                  or alphabetical order.

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Execute rollout site by site or team by team

Step 1 — Execute the rollout wave

Deploying wave by wave, rather than all sites simultaneously, keeps
support capacity matched to demand and lets lessons from wave 1 improve
wave 2.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process        Outputs        Customers

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Retire the old process formally at each site

revert to the familiar path under pressure.

Failure Modes to Watch For — Business Process Reengineering

-   Redesign anchored to the old process — the most common BPR failure
    mode is a “redesign” that quietly preserves the old process's
    structure because the team never truly worked from a blank page.
    Guard against this explicitly in P2 by requiring the design team to
    justify every element of the new process against the design
    principles, not against “how it works today.”

-   Pilot success that doesn't scale — a pilot run by an unusually
    strong team or site can validate a design that fails once rolled out
    to average-performing teams; select the pilot site for
    representativeness, not for its best people.

5.2 Business Process Automation — Full Track

Phase Sequence (Mapped to the Common Lifecycle)

-   Automation-Opportunity Assessment (process mining) — maps to P1

-   Automation Architecture Design (RPA / AI / Human / Integration) —
    maps to P2

-   Build (bots, AI integration, system integration — three parallel
    tracks) — maps to P3

-   UAT & Shadow-Mode Validation (human review of every automated
    decision) — maps to P4

-   Production Go-Live (stepped-down human review) — maps to P5

-   Exception-Handling Tuning & Hypercare — maps to P6

-   Center-of-Excellence Handover — maps to P7

Typical Timeline

3–9 months per workflow, driven primarily by the number of systems and
AI components involved.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Mine the process and classify steps by actor type

Step 1 — Mine and classify the process

Process mining reconstructs what actually happens from system logs, not
what a procedure document says should happen — a distinction that
matters enormously once automation is on the table.

SIPOC for this step.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process        Outputs          Customers
  -------------- ---------------- -------------- ---------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Strategic        Process mining Classified       Steering
  Lead;          automation                      process-mining   Committee;
  Functional     mandate; system                 output           Automation
  Process Owner; access for                                       design team;
  Executive      process-mining                                   Change
  Sponsor.       tools;                                           Manager.
                 historical                                       
                 transaction                                      
                 logs.                                            

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Process mining

  Technique Goal  Automatically reconstruct the real process flow from
                  system event logs and classify each step.

  Technique       Run process-mining software against 90 days of
  Details         transaction logs, then classify each discovered step as
                  rules-based (RPA candidate), judgment-based (AI/human
                  candidate), or an integration point.

  Recommended     Apache NiFi (log extraction) + a process-mining tool
  Open-Source     
  Tool            
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Quantify volume, error rate and cost per transaction

Step 1 — Quantify the automation business case

The business case for automation lives or dies on volume and error-rate
numbers; an automation candidate with low volume rarely justifies the
build cost regardless of how tedious it feels.

SIPOC for this step.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process                Outputs        Customers
  -------------- ---------------- ---------------------- -------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Strategic        Data profiling and     Quantified     Steering
  Lead;          automation       cost-per-transaction   automation     Committee;
  Functional     mandate; system  analysis               business case  Automation
  Process Owner; access for                                             design team;
  Executive      process-mining                                         Change
  Sponsor.       tools;                                                 Manager.
                 historical                                             
                 transaction                                            
                 logs.                                                  

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Data profiling and cost-per-transaction analysis

  Technique Goal  Establish a defensible, quantified case for automation
                  investment.

  Technique       Profile transaction volume, manual error rate, and
  Details         fully loaded cost per transaction for each shortlisted
                  step.

  Recommended     OpenRefine
  Open-Source     
  Tool            
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Shortlist the highest-value automation candidates

Step 1 — Shortlist automation candidates

Scoring feasibility alongside value prevents the common trap of
committing first to the highest-value candidate that also happens to be
the hardest technically, stalling the whole program's early momentum.

SIPOC for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process               Outputs        Customers
  -------------- ---------------- --------------------- -------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Strategic        Opportunity-scoring   Scored and     Steering
  Lead;          automation       workshop              sequenced      Committee;
  Functional     mandate; system                        automation     Automation
  Process Owner; access for                             roadmap        design team;
  Executive      process-mining                                        Change
  Sponsor.       tools;                                                Manager.
                 historical                                            
                 transaction                                           
                 logs.                                                 

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Opportunity-scoring workshop

  Technique Goal  Rank candidate steps by value and feasibility to
                  sequence the automation roadmap.

  Technique       Score each candidate on value (volume × cost × error
  Details         rate) and feasibility (system access, data quality,
                  judgment complexity), then sequence the roadmap
                  accordingly.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc
  Open-Source     
  Tool

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Pulse survey

                  job-security anxiety becomes visible.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Design the end-to-end automation architecture

Step 1 — Design the automation architecture

The architecture is the single artifact that makes this program's “full
track” nature explicit — every step in the workflow needs a named actor
type, with no ambiguous steps left undesigned.

SIPOC for this step.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process                   Outputs         Customers
  -------------- ---------------- ------------------------- --------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Classified       Automation-architecture   Automation      Build team
  Lead; AI/ML    process-mining   design workshop           architecture    (next phase);
  lead (if       output; scored                             diagram and     Steering
  applicable);   automation                                 specification   Committee;
  Functional     roadmap; system                                            Change
  Process Owner. landscape                                                  Manager.
                 documentation.                                             

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Automation-architecture design workshop

  Technique Goal  Specify exactly which steps are RPA, which are
                  AI-assisted, which stay human, and how they hand off to
                  each other.

  Technique       Diagram the end-to-end workflow with every step tagged
  Details         RPA, AI, human, or integration, and every handoff
                  between actor types explicitly specified.

  Recommended     draw.io
  Open-Source     
  Tool            
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Design the human-in-the-loop review model

Step 1 — Design the human-review model

A full-track design without an explicit human-review model tends to
either over-automate (removing judgment where it's genuinely needed) or
under-automate (keeping unnecessary manual review); this task forces the
tradeoff into the open.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process             Outputs             Customers
  -------------- ---------------- ------------------- ------------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Classified       Human-in-the-loop   Human-in-the-loop   Build team
  Lead; AI/ML    process-mining   design              review design       (next phase);
  lead (if       output; scored                                           Steering
  applicable);   automation                                               Committee;
  Functional     roadmap; system                                          Change
  Process Owner. landscape                                                Manager.
                 documentation.                                           

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Human-in-the-loop design

  Technique Goal  Define exactly when and how a human reviews or
                  overrides an automated decision.

  Technique       Define review triggers (confidence threshold,
  Details         transaction value, exception type) and the review
                  interface humans will use.

  Recommended     draw.io
  Open-Source     
  Tool            
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Specify the system-integration contracts

Step 1 — Specify the integration contracts

Every integration contract agreed now, on a whiteboard, is a production
incident avoided later; skipping this step to save time is the single
most common cause of a BPA program's first serious defect.

SIPOC for this step.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process        Outputs          Customers
  -------------- ---------------- -------------- ---------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Classified       Integration    Interface        Build team
  Lead; AI/ML    process-mining   design         contract         (next phase);
  lead (if       output; scored   workshop       specifications   Steering
  applicable);   automation                                       Committee;
  Functional     roadmap; system                                  Change
  Process Owner. landscape                                        Manager.
                 documentation.                                   

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Integration design workshop

  Technique Goal  Agree interface contracts between the automation
                  platform and every system it touches.

  Technique       Whiteboard every inbound/outbound interface the
  Details         automation platform needs, agreeing format, frequency
                  and error-handling with each owning system's technical
                  lead.

  Recommended     draw.io
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 4 — [CHANGE] Launch automation-phase communications

Step 1 — Communicate the automation plan

Silence on job security in an automation program is never neutral — it

communication has to say plainly what is and isn't changing about
people's roles.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Build the RPA bots

Step 1 — Build the RPA bots

Building bots one at a time, with unit tests at each stage, catches a
broken assumption about the underlying system's UI or data format before
it propagates into the integrated workflow.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs              Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- ------------------- -------------- -------------- --------------

  developers;    human-in-the-loop                  RPA bots       Technical
  AI/ML          review design;                                    Lead; Change
  engineers;     integration                                       Manager.
  integration    contracts.                                        
  developers.                                                      

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Iterative bot development

  Technique Goal  Build and unit-test each RPA bot against the automation
                  architecture.

  Technique       Develop each bot in 2-week increments, with unit tests
  Details         run against a non-production copy of each target system
                  after every increment.

  Recommended     OpenProject (backlog) + an open-source RPA framework
  Open-Source

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Integrate or fine-tune the AI/ML components

Step 1 — Integrate the AI/ML components

An AI component's accuracy is only as good as the data it's tuned
against; this task explicitly includes fine-tuning on the organization's
own historical decisions, not just deploying a generic model.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs              Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- ------------------- -------------- -------------- --------------

  developers.                                                      

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  AI model integration and fine-tuning

  Technique Goal  Connect or fine-tune the AI/ML model against the
                  automation architecture's judgment-based steps.

  Technique       Integrate the selected model, fine-tune it against a
  Details         labeled sample of historical decisions, and document
                  its confidence-threshold behavior for the human-review
                  design.

  Recommended     Open-source ML framework (e.g., scikit-learn / Hugging
  Open-Source     Face)

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Build the system integrations

Step 1 — Build the integrations

Building each integration strictly against its written contract, rather
than against an assumed “how the other system probably works,” is what
makes the later automated testing meaningful rather than a formality.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs              Process              Outputs        Customers
  -------------- ------------------- -------------------- -------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Automation          System-integration   Built and      UAT team (next
  Lead; RPA      architecture;       development          tested system  phase); IT /
  developers;    human-in-the-loop                        integrations   Technical
  AI/ML          review design;                                          Lead; Change
  engineers;     integration                                             Manager.
  integration    contracts.                                              
  developers.                                                            

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  System-integration development

  Technique Goal  Build each integration against the contracts specified
                  in Phase 2.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Run UAT against the full automated workflow

Step 1 — Run UAT on the automated workflow

UAT here has to test the handoffs between actor types, not just each bot
or integration in isolation — the most common defects in a full-track
program occur exactly at those handoff boundaries.

SIPOC for this step.

                   monitoring                                    Manager.
                   plan.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Scripted test execution

  Technique Goal  Validate the full RPA/AI/human/integration workflow
                  against acceptance criteria.

  Technique       Execute test scripts covering the full workflow end to
  Details         end, with particular attention to the handoff points
                  between RPA, AI, and human steps.
