Task 2 — [JOINT] Run a human-reviewed shadow-mode period

Step 1 — Run the shadow-mode period

Shadow mode is what separates a genuinely validated automation from a
hopeful one — every decision the AI or bot makes is checked by a human
before anything is trusted to run unsupervised.

SIPOC for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers        Inputs          Process        Outputs        Customers
  ---------------- --------------- -------------- -------------- --------------
  IT / Technical   Built bots, AI  Shadow-mode    Shadow-mode    Production
  Lead; Functional components and  validation     review log     support team
  Process Owner; a integrations;                                 (next phase);
  representative   UAT test                                      Steering
  sample of end    scripts;                                      Committee;
  users.           shadow-mode                                   Change
                   monitoring                                    Manager.
                   plan.                                         

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Shadow-mode validation

  Technique Goal  Run the automated workflow alongside the manual
                  process, with every automated decision reviewed by a
                  human before it counts.

  Technique       Run the automated workflow in parallel with the
  Details         existing manual process for a defined period (commonly
                  2–4 weeks), with a human reviewing and confirming every
                  automated decision.

  Recommended     Kiwi TCMS (tracking)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Measure automated-decision accuracy against human
judgment

Step 1 — Measure automation accuracy

Phase 5 — without it, the step-down decision in production becomes a
guess rather than an evidence-based threshold.

  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Phased review step-down

                  sustained production accuracy, not a single day's data.

  Details         gated on sustained production accuracy matching or
                  exceeding the shadow-mode threshold.

Task 5 — [JOINT] Activate elevated support on Day 1

Step 1 — Staff elevated support

Exception volume in an automation go-live tends to spike in a different
pattern than a typical system go-live — concentrated around edge cases
the shadow mode didn't happen to cover — so support staffing should
include someone who can triage automation-specific exceptions, not just
general help-desk staff.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------

  authority).    human-review                                 
                 step-down                                    
                 plan.                                        

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Elevated help-desk staffing

  Technique Goal  Absorb the go-live question and exception-handling
                  spike.

  Technique       Elevate help-desk and technical support staffing for
  Details         the first five business days, with a named escalation
                  path for automation-specific exceptions.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Track production accuracy and exception volume daily

Step 1 — Track accuracy and exceptions daily

A dashboard that separates “exception volume” from “accuracy” matters
because a rising exception count with stable accuracy usually means a
data-quality issue upstream, not a failing automation.

Task 5 — [JOINT] Complete the human-review step-down to steady state

Step 1 — Complete the review step-down

The final step-down should require the longest sustained evidence window
of the whole staged process, since it is the last checkpoint before
human oversight reaches its permanent steady-state level.

                 triggers.

  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Evidence-gated step-down completion

  Technique Goal  Confirm the final human-review level is justified by
                  sustained production accuracy.

  Technique       Confirm sustained accuracy at or above threshold for a
  Details         defined period before moving to the final, steady-state
                  human-review level.

Step 1 — Stand up the Center of Excellence

Bots and AI models degrade or drift without maintenance in a way a
static process change does not — this is why automation uniquely
requires a permanent owning team, not just a project handover document.

Failure Modes to Watch For — Business Process Automation — Full Track

-   Treating the bot/AI as invisible infrastructure rather than a
    “worker” with an owner — without a named accountable role for each
    automated step, a failing bot or drifting AI model can run
    undetected for weeks; the RACSI in this guide deliberately assigns
    Accountable to a named human role for every automated step, never to
    “the system.”

-   Stepping down human review too early — reducing human-in-the-loop
    oversight before the accuracy threshold is genuinely proven, usually
    under delivery-timeline pressure, is the most common cause of a BPA
    program's first serious production incident.

5.3 Integrated Management System (e.g., QHSE)

Phase Sequence (Mapped to the Common Lifecycle)

-   Intake (certification requirement confirmed) — maps to P1

-   Diagnosis (gap assessment against the standard) — maps to P1

-   Design (management-system documentation) — maps to P2

-   Implementation (procedures, records, controls in daily operation) —
    maps to P3

-   Mock-up Audit (internal pre-assessment) — maps to P4

-   Certifying Audit (external, accredited certification body) — maps to
    P5

-   Surveillance Cycle (ongoing internal and external audits) — maps to
    P6–P7

Typical Timeline

6–14 months to first certification; then an ongoing annual
surveillance-audit cycle indefinitely.

5.3.1 P1 — Intake — Certification Requirement Confirmed

Typically the first 2–3 weeks of a 6–14 month program.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Assess current practice against every clause of the
standard

Step 1 — Assess practice against the standard

Skipping a clause because it “probably doesn't apply” is how a program
discovers a serious gap at the mock-up audit instead of here, at
diagnosis, when it is far cheaper to fix.

SIPOC for this step.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers          Inputs          Process            Outputs            Customers
  ------------------ --------------- ------------------ ------------------ --------------
  Functional Process Confirmed       Clause-by-clause   Clause-by-clause   Design team
  Owners; the        certification   gap assessment     gap assessment     (next phase);
  certification body requirement;                                          Steering
  (informally, for   certification                                         Committee;
  clause             scope                                                 Change
  interpretation);   statement;                                            Manager.
  Change Manager.    current QHSE                                          
                     documentation                                         
                     and practices.                                        

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Clause-by-clause gap assessment

  Technique Goal  Systematically check current practice against every
                  applicable clause of the target standard.

  Technique       Walk every clause of the standard against current
  Details         documented and observed practice, using the standard's
                  own structure as the assessment checklist.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc (checklist template)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Quantify the gap by clause and by risk

Step 1 — Quantify gaps by risk

Not every gap carries equal weight; a missing record-retention procedure
and an unmitigated safety hazard both count as “gaps” but demand very
different urgency.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers          Inputs          Process        Outputs        Customers
  ------------------ --------------- -------------- -------------- --------------

  (informally, for   certification                                 Committee;
  clause             scope                                         Change
  interpretation);   statement;                                    Manager.
  Change Manager.    current QHSE                                  
                     documentation                                 
                     and practices.                                

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Risk-based gap scoring

  Technique Goal  Score every identified gap by likelihood and severity
                  of the risk it represents.

  Technique       Score each gap on likelihood and severity using a
  Details         standard risk matrix, producing a ranked gap register.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc
  Open-Source

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Design the management-system manual

Step 1 — Design the management-system manual

Structuring the manual to mirror the standard's own clause numbering,
rather than the organization's internal department structure, is what
makes the eventual audit efficient rather than a scavenger hunt.

SIPOC for this step.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process        Outputs             Customers

  document       standard's     clauses                            Process Owners;
  control lead.  clause                                            Change Manager.
                 structure.                                        

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Manual authoring, mapped to standard clauses

  Technique Goal  Produce the top-level manual an auditor uses to
                  navigate the whole system.

  Technique       Draft the management-system manual with a
  Details         clause-by-clause cross-reference table linking each
                  clause to its governing procedure.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Writer
  Open-Source

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Design procedures and work instructions for each gap

Step 1 — Draft procedures for each gap

A procedure copied from a generic template rarely survives contact with
an auditor's questions about how it actually applies to this
organization's specific operations; drafting with the actual process
owner is what avoids that.

SIPOC for this step.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process              Outputs        Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------------- -------------- ----------------
  Functional     Ranked gap     Procedure-drafting   Drafted        Implementation
  Process        register;      workshop             procedures and team (next
  Owners; Change remediation                         work           phase);
  Manager;       roadmap; the                        instructions   Functional
  document       standard's                                         Process Owners;
  control lead.  clause                                             Change Manager.
                 structure.                                         

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Procedure-drafting workshop

  Technique Goal  Convert each item in the gap register into a specific,
                  workable procedure or work instruction.

  Technique       Draft each procedure directly with the process owner
  Details         who will actually follow it, not solely by a compliance
                  writer working from the standard's text.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Writer
  Open-Source

Task 1 — [JOINT] Roll out procedures and work instructions into daily
operations

the floor, is exactly the gap a certifying auditor is trained to find
through direct observation and interview.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Run the mock-up audit exactly like the real
certifying audit

Step 1 — Run the mock-up audit

The value of a mock-up audit comes entirely from its realism — an
auditor (internal or third-party) who is too gentle produces false
confidence that the certifying audit will not validate.

SIPOC for this step.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process         Outputs        Customers
  -------------- -------------- --------------- -------------- --------------
  Internal or    Closed         Simulated       Completed      Certifying
  third-party    internal audit certification   mock-up audit  audit (next
  mock-up        findings; live audit                          phase);
  auditor;       procedures and                                Steering
  Functional     records; the                                  Committee;
  Process        standard's                                    Change
  Owners; Change audit                                         Manager.
  Manager.       protocol.                                     

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Simulated certification audit

  Technique Goal  Test the system under audit conditions that mirror the
                  real certifying audit as closely as possible.

  Technique       Engage an auditor (internal, cross-trained from another
  Details         site, or third-party) to run the full audit protocol —
                  document review, interviews, floor observation —
                  exactly as the certifying body would.

  Recommended     Kiwi TCMS (adapted as an audit tracker)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Log every nonconformity and observation

Step 1 — Log nonconformities and observations

A minor observation ignored now is a documented pattern a certifying
auditor may treat more seriously if it recurs — nothing surfaced in the
mock-up audit should be dismissed as too small to log.

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Remediate every nonconformity before the certifying
audit

Step 1 — Remediate the mock-up audit findings

Closing a nonconformity means more than fixing the immediate instance —
the standard expects a documented root cause and a corrective action
that prevents recurrence, which is what a certifying auditor will
actually check.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  -------------- -------------- -------------- ------------------- --------------

  Process        standard's                                        Change
  Owners; Change audit                                             Manager.
  Manager.       protocol.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Findings remediation sprint

  Technique Goal  Close every nonconformity with documented corrective
                  action before the certifying audit.

  Technique       Assign an owner and a documented corrective action to
  Details         every finding, with evidence of closure recorded before
                  the certifying audit.

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 7.

Step 1 — Run the Divergence Pattern check

A staff member who can recite the new procedure flawlessly during the
mock-up audit while privately resenting “all this paperwork” is a
compliance-without-commitment risk likely to regress once audit
attention passes.

  Details         with Bridges = Ending) against mock-up audit

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Host the certifying audit (Stage 1 and Stage 2)

Step 1 — Host the certifying audit

The organization's role here is to make evidence easy to find and staff
easy to interview — not to manage or steer the auditor's independent
judgment, which the standard explicitly requires to remain unbiased.

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Respond to any nonconformities raised during the
audit

Step 1 — Respond to audit nonconformities

Certifying auditors commonly allow a defined window to submit
corrective-action evidence for minor nonconformities without failing
certification outright — responding within that window, thoroughly, is
what preserves the certification timeline.

  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Real-time nonconformity response

  Details         nonconformity raised, submitted within the

Task 5 — [JOINT] Activate elevated support through the audit period

Step 1 — Support staff through the audit

Even well-prepared staff often feel unusual pressure being directly
interviewed by an external auditor; a visible support presence during
the audit reduces the chance that nervousness alone produces a
misleading answer.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers       Inputs             Process        Outputs        Customers
  --------------- ------------------ -------------- -------------- --------------
  Accredited      Closed mock-up     Elevated       Active on-site All staff;

Task 1 — [JOINT] Embed the management system into standard operations

Step 1 — Embed the system into BAU

A management system that survives as a separate compliance exercise,
disconnected from daily operational management, is at high risk of
quietly decaying between surveillance audits.

  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Operational integration review

  Technique Goal  Ensure the management system's procedures are simply
                  “how work is done,” not a parallel compliance activity.

  Technique       Review whether procedures are referenced in standard
  Details         operational meetings and management reviews, not only
                  in dedicated QHSE meetings.

Failure Modes to Watch For — Integrated Management System (e.g., QHSE)

-   Documentation without daily practice — a management system that
    exists as a polished binder but isn't actually how work happens on
    the floor will fail a certifying audit's interview and observation
    stage even if every document looks correct; use the mock-up audit
    specifically to test practice, not paperwork.

-   Treating the certifying audit as a one-time event — the
    certification is only the entry point to a standing
    surveillance-audit cycle; a program plan that ends at certification,
    without a resourced ongoing internal-audit function, sets up a
    failed surveillance audit within a year or two.

5.4 Cultural / Values Transformation

Phase Sequence (Mapped to the Common Lifecycle)

-   Culture Diagnosis (survey + behavioral observation) — maps to P1

-   Target Values & Behavior Design — maps to P2

-   Leadership Modeling & Reinforcement-Infrastructure Build — maps to
    P3

-   Pilot Cohort Validation — maps to P4

-   Organization-Wide Rollout — maps to P5

-   Reinforcement Through Skepticism (Stabilization) — maps to P6

-   Institutionalization into Standing Practice — maps to P7

Typical Timeline

18–36 months to embed durably — the longest realistic timeline of any
type in this guide.

5.4.1 P1 — Culture Diagnosis — Survey + Behavioral Observation

Typically the first 2–3 months of an 18–36 month program.

Task 1 — [CHANGE] Survey the organization's current culture

Step 1 — Survey the current culture

Asking people what the organization's values actually feel like day to
day, rather than whether they can recite the values statement, is what
surfaces the real gap this program exists to close.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Culture survey

  Technique Goal  Quantify how employees currently perceive the
                  organization's actual (not stated) values.

  Technique       Distribute a survey asking employees to describe
  Details         behaviors they observe, not just to rate agreement with
                  the stated values.

Task 2 — [CHANGE] Observe actual behavior against stated values

Step 1 — Observe behavior directly

Survey data alone is rarely enough here — people are often unreliable
narrators of their own culture, and direct observation of how a meeting
is actually run reveals things a survey question cannot.

                  interactions for evidence of lived versus stated
                  values.

  Details         sample of meetings and decisions across levels and

Task 2 — [CHANGE] Design the leadership-modeling plan

Step 1 — Design leadership modeling

Culture change spreads from what leaders visibly do, not from what any
communication says — which is why this plan has to specify particular,
observable leadership actions, not general commitment.

SIPOC for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process               Outputs               Customers

                 for culture.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Leadership-modeling plan design

  Technique Goal  Specify exactly how senior leaders will visibly
                  demonstrate the target behaviors first.

  Technique       Work with each senior leader to identify specific,
  Details         near-term situations where they will visibly model a
                  target behavior.

Task 3 — [CHANGE] Identify the systemic changes needed to support the
target behaviors

Step 1 — Identify systemic changes required

A target behavior that is not reflected in what gets someone promoted or
recognized is competing against a stronger, silent signal — this
analysis exists to find and fix that mismatch.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process             Outputs             Customers
  -------------- -------------- ------------------- ------------------- ---------------
  Executive      Synthesized    Systems-alignment   Systems-alignment   Reinforcement
  Sponsor;       culture-gap    analysis            gap analysis        build team
  leadership     statement;                                             (next phase);
  team; HR /     strategic                                              Steering
  People         direction;                                             Committee;
  function.      leadership's                                           Change Manager.
                 own                                                    
                 aspirations                                            
                 for culture.                                           

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Systems-alignment analysis

  Technique Goal  Identify which existing systems (performance review,
                  promotion, recognition) currently reward the opposite
                  of the target behaviors.

  Technique       Review performance-review criteria, promotion criteria,
  Details         and recognition programs against the target behaviors,
                  flagging every misalignment.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc
  Open-Source

Task 1 — [CHANGE] Build recognition mechanisms tied to target behaviors

Step 1 — Build the recognition mechanisms

A recognition mechanism that stays generic (“employee of the month”)
rather than naming the specific target behavior being rewarded misses
the chance to teach the behavior by example every time it's used.

SIPOC for this step.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs                Process               Outputs             Customers
  -------------- --------------------- --------------------- ------------------- --------------
  HR / People    Leadership-modeling   Recognition-program   Live,               Pilot cohort
  function;      plan;                 design                behavior-specific   (next phase);
  Executive      systems-alignment gap                       recognition         HR / People
  Sponsor;       analysis;                                   mechanism           function;
  leadership     recognition-program                                             Change
  team.          design inputs.                                                  Manager.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Recognition-program design

  Technique Goal  Create visible, specific recognition tied directly to
                  demonstrated target behaviors.

  Technique       Design a recognition mechanism (spot awards, peer
  Details         nominations, leadership shout-outs) that explicitly
                  names the target behavior being recognized each time.

  Recommended     BookStack (recognition wall / log)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Align performance-review criteria to target behaviors

Step 1 — Revise performance-review criteria

This is the single highest-leverage systemic change available to a
culture program — what gets measured in a performance review is what
most reliably shapes daily behavior over time.

SIPOC for this step.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs                Process        Outputs              Customers
  -------------- --------------------- -------------- -------------------- --------------
  HR / People    Leadership-modeling   HR policy      Revised              Pilot cohort
  function;      plan;                 integration    performance-review   (next phase);
  Executive      systems-alignment gap                criteria             HR / People
  Sponsor;       analysis;                                                 function;
  leadership     recognition-program                                       Change
  team.          design inputs.                                            Manager.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  HR policy integration

  Technique Goal  Embed target behaviors into formal performance-review
                  criteria.

  Technique       Work with HR to revise performance-review criteria and
  Details         manager guidance to explicitly assess target behaviors.

  Recommended     BookStack (policy documentation)
  Open-Source

Step 1 — Launch leadership modeling

The first few visible instances of leadership genuinely modeling a
target behavior — especially in a difficult moment, not an easy one — do
more to build credibility than months of subsequent communication.

Task 5 — [CHANGE] Log barrier reasons from skepticism about genuine
commitment

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 6.

Step 1 — Log the barrier reasons

“This is just corporate talk” is the most common barrier reason in a
culture program's early months; the specific evidence behind that
skepticism (a past failed initiative, a specific leader's behavior) is
what the response needs to address directly.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs                Process          Outputs          Customers
  -------------- --------------------- ---------------- ---------------- --------------
  HR / People    Leadership-modeling   Barrier-reason   Logged           Pilot cohort
  function;      plan;                 logging          barrier-reason   (next phase);
  Executive      systems-alignment gap                  records          HR / People
  Sponsor;       analysis;                                               function;
  leadership     recognition-program                                     Change
  team.          design inputs.                                          Manager.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Barrier-reason logging

  Technique Goal  Capture the specific, named reason behind any Desire ≤
                  2 score.

  Technique       For every cohort scoring Desire at 2 or below, require
  Details         a one-sentence, named barrier reason before the score
                  is accepted.

  Recommended     OpenProject (custom field)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 1 — [JOINT] Select and brief the pilot cohort or business unit

Step 1 — Select a representative pilot cohort

Selecting the most enthusiastic team as the pilot produces a validation
that will not survive contact with the organization's more skeptical
majority; representativeness matters as much here as in any other
transformation type in this guide.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs                Process              Outputs        Customers
  -------------- --------------------- -------------------- -------------- -------------------
  Pilot business Live recognition      Representativeness   Selected pilot Steering Committee;
  unit or        mechanisms; revised   screening            cohort with    organization-wide
  cohort; HR /   performance-review                         documented     rollout team (next
  People         criteria;                                  rationale      phase); Change
  function;      leadership-modeling                                       Manager.
  Change         examples.                                                 
  Manager.                                                                 

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Representativeness screening

  Technique Goal  Choose a pilot cohort that reflects average
                  organizational conditions, not the most receptive team
                  available.

  Technique       Score candidate cohorts against representativeness
  Details         criteria (baseline sentiment, leadership engagement,
                  size) before selecting the pilot.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 2 — [CHANGE] Run the reinforcement plan within the pilot cohort

Step 1 — Run the reinforcement plan

Running the full reinforcement plan together, rather than testing
individual mechanisms separately, is what validates whether they work in
combination — which is how they will actually operate at full rollout.

  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Pilot reinforcement operation

  Technique Goal  Operate recognition, review-criteria and
                  leadership-modeling mechanisms within the pilot cohort
                  for a defined period.

  Technique       Operate the recognition mechanism, revised review
  Details         criteria, and active leadership modeling within the
                  pilot cohort for 2–3 months.

  Recommended     BookStack; Mattermost

Task 4 — [CHANGE] Cross-check Knowledge / Ability against Bridges
(Divergence Pattern check)

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 7.

Step 1 — Run the Divergence Pattern check

In a culture program this pattern often shows up as someone who has
learned to say the right things in front of leadership while privately
still operating by the old norms — exactly the
compliance-without-commitment risk that undermines a wider rollout.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs                Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- --------------------- -------------- -------------- -------------------
  Pilot business Live recognition      Divergence     Divergence     Steering Committee;
  unit or        mechanisms; revised   Pattern review Pattern alert  organization-wide
  cohort; HR /   performance-review                   list           rollout team (next
  People         criteria;                                           phase); Change
  function;      leadership-modeling                                 Manager.
  Change         examples.                                           
  Manager.                                                           

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Divergence Pattern review

  Technique Goal  Flag pilot participants who can recite the new
                  behaviors but whose Bridges reading is still at Ending.

  Technique       Run the automated rule (Knowledge ≥ 3 and Ability ≥ 3
  Details         with Bridges = Ending) against pilot cohort scores.

  Recommended     Metabase (rule-based alert)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 5 — [PROJECT] Confirm go/no-go readiness for organization-wide
rollout

Step 1 — Confirm rollout readiness

The go/no-go here should weigh genuine behavioral movement over
survey-score movement alone, since a self-reported score can shift
faster than real behavior does.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs                Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- --------------------- -------------- -------------- -------------------

  People         criteria;                            decision       phase); Change
  function;      leadership-modeling                                 Manager.
  Change         examples.                                           
  Manager.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Formal go/no-go workshop

  Technique Goal  Gain explicit Sponsor sign-off that the reinforcement
                  plan is validated for wider rollout.

  Technique       Present the pilot before/after comparison to the
  Details         Sponsor; record an explicit go / no-go / extend-pilot
                  decision.

  Recommended     OpenProject (decision log)

Step 1 — Sequence the rollout

Sequencing by leadership engagement, rather than a simple org-chart
order, means the waves most likely to succeed go first, building
credible proof points for more skeptical units that follow.

Task 4 — [CHANGE] Call Refreeze formally and provisionally, given
ongoing regression risk

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 4.

Step 1 — Confirm the provisional Refreeze call

Unlike most other transformation types in this guide, culture Refreeze
should be recorded as provisional by design — the honest, evidence-based
position is that regression risk stays real for years after the formal
program closes.

Failure Modes to Watch For — Cultural / Values Transformation

-   Values as posters, not practice — the single most common failure
    mode is publishing new values language without changing what gets
    rewarded, promoted, or tolerated; if the
    reinforcement-infrastructure build in P3 is skipped or
    under-resourced, the rest of the program becomes theater.

-   Declaring victory at the pilot — a successful pilot cohort proves
    the design can work under supportive, closely watched conditions; it
    does not prove the reinforcement mechanisms will hold once
    leadership attention moves elsewhere during the long
    organization-wide rollout.

5.5 Operating Model Redesign

Phase Sequence (Mapped to the Common Lifecycle)

-   Current Operating Model Assessment — maps to P1

-   Target Operating Model (TOM) Design — maps to P2

-   Detailed Org Design & Transition Planning — maps to P3

-   Pilot Transition (one function or region) — maps to P4

-   Full Organizational Transition — maps to P5

-   New Governance Adoption Tracking — maps to P6

-   Standing Operating Rhythm Handover — maps to P7

Typical Timeline

9–18 months from diagnosis to full transition, depending on
organizational scale.

5.5.1 P1 — Current Operating Model Assessment

Typically the first 4–6 weeks of a 9–18 month program.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Assess the current org structure and governance

Step 1 — Assess the current structure

The org chart shows the formal structure; the interview program is what
reveals the informal structure — who actually gets consulted, which
meetings actually decide things — that any redesign has to account for.

SIPOC for this step.

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers        Inputs             Process        Outputs         Customers
  ---------------- ------------------ -------------- --------------- --------------
  Executive        Strategic mandate  Structured     Current-state   Steering
  Sponsor;         for redesign;      interview      structure and   Committee;
  function heads;  current org charts program        governance map  function
  Enterprise /     and governance                                    heads; Change
  organizational   documentation;                                    Manager.
  strategy team.   cross-functional                                  
                   friction reports.                                 

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Structured interview program

  Technique Goal  Map the current org structure, governance forums, and
                  how decisions actually get made.

  Technique       Run structured interviews across every affected
  Details         function, mapping both the formal reporting structure
                  and the informal decision-making patterns.

  Recommended     Taguette (interview coding)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Assess current decision rights and capability gaps

Step 1 — Assess decision rights and capability

Persistent cross-functional friction is frequently a symptom of unclear
decision rights rather than a personality conflict; this assessment
tests that hypothesis explicitly rather than assuming it.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers        Inputs             Process           Outputs           Customers
  ---------------- ------------------ ----------------- ----------------- --------------
  Executive        Strategic mandate  Decision-rights   Decision-rights   Steering
  Sponsor;         for redesign;      and capability    and capability    Committee;
  function heads;  current org charts assessment        gap analysis      function
  Enterprise /     and governance                                         heads; Change
  organizational   documentation;                                         Manager.
  strategy team.   cross-functional                                       
                   friction reports.                                      

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Decision-rights and capability assessment

  Technique Goal  Identify where decision rights are unclear or
                  mismatched to capability.

  Technique       Map who currently has authority for each major
  Details         recurring decision type, and assess whether the
                  function holding that authority has the capability to
                  exercise it well.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc (decision-rights matrix)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Quantify the cost of cross-functional friction

A specific, sourced estimate of delayed decisions or duplicated work
carries far more weight in a redesign business case than a general sense
that “coordination feels hard.”

  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------

                  structure's cross-functional friction.

  Technique       Quantify delayed decisions, duplicated work, and
  Details         escalation volume attributable to structural friction,
                  using time-tracking or workflow data where available.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Develop candidate TOM options

Step 1 — Develop TOM options

Presenting the Steering Committee with a single option to approve
invites a rubber-stamp decision; two or three genuinely distinct,
credible options force a real evaluation of tradeoffs.

SIPOC for this step.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers        Inputs            Process           Outputs        Customers
  ---------------- ----------------- ----------------- -------------- --------------
  Executive        Current operating Operating-model   Candidate TOM  Detailed
  Sponsor;         model assessment; design workshop   options with   design team
  function heads;  decision-rights                     tradeoff       (next phase);
  Enterprise /     and capability                      analysis       Steering
  organizational   gap analysis;                                      Committee;
  strategy team.   strategic                                          Change
                   direction.                                         Manager.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Operating-model design workshop

  Technique Goal  Develop a small number of credible target operating
                  model options before choosing one.

  Technique       Facilitate a design session producing 2–3 distinct TOM
  Details         options, each with an explicit statement of its
                  tradeoffs.

  Recommended     draw.io
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Select and detail the preferred TOM

Step 1 — Select and detail the TOM

The selection criteria should be agreed before the options are
presented, not derived after the fact to justify whichever option
leadership already favored — that sequencing protects the decision's
credibility.

                  working level.

  Technique       Present the candidate options against pre-agreed
  Details         selection criteria to the Steering Committee, then
                  detail the selected option's structure, governance and
                  capabilities.

org chart alone, without explicit decision rights, tends to reproduce

  Details         roles for every major recurring decision type
                  identified in the P1 assessment.

Task 4 — [CHANGE] Launch TOM-design communications

Step 1 — Communicate the TOM direction

Structural redesign communications need unusual honesty about what is
and is not yet decided — overpromising certainty this early, then having
to walk it back, does lasting damage to Desire.

                   direction.                                          Manager.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Communications planning

  Technique Goal  Explain the direction of the redesign before
                  speculation about specific job impacts fills the gap.

  Technique       Build a communications calendar that is explicit about
  Details         what is confirmed, what is still being decided, and
                  when more detail will be available.

  Recommended     Listmonk
  Open-Source

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 5.

Step 1 — Recruit and brief champions

Because structural change affects functions unevenly, champions
distributed across every affected function — not concentrated in one —
give the clearest early picture of where anxiety is highest.

  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Nomination survey & champion kickoff workshop

  Technique Goal  Stand up a peer network that can surface
                  structural-change anxiety early, function by function.

  Technique       Nominate 1 champion per 15–20 employees across every
  Details         affected function, then brief them on the TOM direction
                  and their escalation path.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Finalize detailed role and reporting-line design

Step 1 — Finalize the detailed role design

This is the point structural redesign becomes concrete and personal —
the same sensitivity that applies to role change in a reengineering
program applies here, magnified by the larger scale.

SIPOC for this step.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Detailed org-design workshop

  Technique Goal  Convert the TOM blueprint into named roles, reporting
                  lines and headcount by function.

  Details         descriptions, reporting lines, and headcount plans
                  under the new structure.

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Build the transition / migration plan

Step 1 — Build the transition plan

Sequencing the transition to protect operational continuity — rather

structural transition from a chaotic one.

SIPOC for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs            Process           Outputs        Customers

                 capability                                         Manager.
                 requirements.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Transition-plan sequencing

  Technique Goal  Sequence how the organization moves from current to
                  target structure with minimal operational disruption.

  Technique       Build a phased migration plan sequencing which
  Details         functions or regions transition first, with explicit
                  operational-continuity safeguards for each phase.

Task 1 — [JOINT] Select and brief the pilot function or region

Step 1 — Select the pilot function or region

Because the pilot transition becomes the reference case every skeptic
will point to, the selection should weigh readiness alongside
representativeness — a representative but poorly led pilot risks
discrediting a sound design.

SIPOC for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process              Outputs           Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------------- ----------------- --------------

  / People       transition     screening            with documented   full
  function;      plan; designed                      rationale         transition
  Change         governance                                            team (next
  Manager.       forums.                                               phase); Change
                                                                       Manager.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Representativeness and readiness screening

  Technique Goal  Choose a pilot that is both representative of the wider
                  organization and has adequate leadership readiness.

  Technique       Score candidate functions or regions on
  Details         representativeness and leadership readiness before
                  selecting the pilot.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc
  Open-Source

transition.

                  competently while still emotionally tied to the old
                  one.

  Details         with Bridges = Ending) against pilot participants'
                  latest scores.

Task 5 — [PROJECT] Remediate pilot findings before full transition
sign-off

Step 1 — Remediate the pilot findings

A governance-forum finding — decisions still being routed the old way —
usually calls for a design or coaching fix, not simply patience; treat
it with the same seriousness as a technical defect in other
transformation types.

SIPOC for this step.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process        Outputs          Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------- ---------------- --------------

  / People       transition     review         pilot-findings   full
  function;      plan; designed                log              transition
  Change         governance                                     team (next
  Manager.       forums.                                        phase); Change
                                                                Manager.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Findings remediation review

  Technique Goal  Close or formally accept every pilot finding before
                  recommending full transition.

  Technique       Triage every pilot finding, closing structural and
  Details         governance issues, and formally accepting minor
                  findings with Sponsor sign-off before full transition.

  Recommended     OpenProject (decision log)
  Open-Source

deserves the same evidentiary rigor as any other type's most
consequential milestone — no different simply because it involves people
rather than technology.

Task 5 — [JOINT] Activate elevated support during the transition

Step 1 — Staff elevated support

Support demand after a structural transition skews heavily toward HR and
role-clarity questions rather than technical ones, so staffing should
reflect that mix, not default to a standard IT help-desk model.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------- -------------- --------------

Failure Modes to Watch For — Operating Model Redesign

-   Structure changes on paper, decisions don't change in practice — the
    org chart updates immediately but decisions quietly keep flowing
    through the old reporting lines out of habit; P6's tracking task
    exists specifically to catch this before it hardens into a permanent
    workaround.

-   Underestimating the pilot's political weight — whichever function or
    region is chosen for the pilot transition becomes the reference case
    every skeptic points to; choose it as carefully as the target-state
    design itself, not simply for logistical convenience.

5.6 Compliance-Driven Change

Phase Sequence (Mapped to the Common Lifecycle)

-   Regulatory Requirement & Gap Analysis — maps to P1

-   Control Design — maps to P2

-   Control Implementation & Mandatory Training — maps to P3

-   Internal Audit / Independent Testing — maps to P4

-   Controls Go Live (enforceable date) — maps to P5

-   First Live Compliance-Monitoring Cycle — maps to P6

-   Ongoing Compliance Monitoring Handover — maps to P7

Typical Timeline

Driven by the external deadline; commonly 3–12 months, with zero
flexibility on the end date.

5.6.1 P1 — Regulatory Requirement & Gap Analysis

Typically the first 2–4 weeks of a 3–12 month program, driven by the
external deadline.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Analyze the regulatory requirement in detail

Step 1 — Analyze the regulatory requirement

A misread regulatory clause discovered during testing, or worse, during
a regulator's own review, is far costlier than the time spent getting
Legal's interpretation right at the outset.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

                 deadline.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Legal / regulatory text analysis

  Technique Goal  Produce a precise, working interpretation of exactly
                  what the regulation requires.

  Technique       Work with Legal or outside counsel to produce a
  Details         clause-by-clause working interpretation of the
                  requirement, with any ambiguous points explicitly
                  flagged.

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Assess current controls against the requirement

Step 1 — Assess current controls

This assessment has to test what the control actually does today, not
what the existing policy document says it should do — the two frequently
diverge under real operating pressure.

SIPOC for this step.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Quantify the control gap and compliance risk

Step 1 — Quantify the compliance risk

Because the deadline is fixed and non-negotiable, risk scoring here
should weight time-to-remediate as heavily as severity — a moderate-risk
gap that takes longer to fix can be more urgent than a higher-risk gap
that's quick to close.

SIPOC for this step.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process        Outputs          Customers
  -------------- ---------------- -------------- ---------------- --------------
  Legal /        The new law,     Risk-based gap Ranked,          Steering
  Compliance     regulation,      scoring        deadline-aware   Committee;
  function;      consent order,                  gap register     Legal /
  Executive      or contractual                                   Compliance;
  Sponsor;       clause; current                                  Change
  Functional     control                                          Manager.
  Process        documentation;                                   
  Owners.        the enforceable                                  
                 deadline.                                        

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Risk-based gap scoring

  Technique Goal  Score every gap by legal, financial and reputational
                  risk if unaddressed by the deadline.

  Technique       Score each gap on severity and estimated remediation
  Details         time, producing a ranked, deadline-aware gap register.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc
  Open-Source     
  Tool            
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Step 1 — Survey the affected population

and Knowledge, not treated as a Desire-stall diagnostic the way it would
be in a discretionary change.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Design the specific controls for each gap

Step 1 — Design the specific controls

A control designed by Legal alone, without the FPO who will actually
operate it, tends to be legally sound but operationally awkward;
designing them together avoids that mismatch from the outset.

SIPOC for this step.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- ---------------- -------------- -------------- ----------------
  Legal /        Ranked,          Control design Designed       Implementation
  Compliance     deadline-aware   workshop       controls per   team (next
  function;      gap register;                   gap            phase); Legal /
  Functional     regulatory                                     Compliance;
  Process        requirement                                    Change Manager.
  Owners; IT /   analysis;                                      
  Technical      existing control                               
  Lead.          architecture.                                  

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Control design workshop

  Technique Goal  Translate each gap into a specific, implementable
                  process, system or policy control.

  Technique       Design each control directly with the FPO who will
  Details         operate it, confirming with Legal that the design
                  genuinely satisfies the regulatory requirement.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Writer
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Map each control back to its governing regulatory
clause

Step 1 — Build the clause-to-control traceability map

This traceability map is frequently the first document a regulator or
auditor asks for — having it maintained from the design stage onward,
rather than reconstructed later, is a meaningful time saving.

SIPOC for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process        Outputs             Customers
  -------------- ---------------- -------------- ------------------- ----------------

  Process        requirement                                         Change Manager.
  Owners; IT /   analysis;                                           
  Technical      existing control                                    
  Lead.          architecture.                                       

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Traceability mapping

  Technique Goal  Create a one-to-one map from each regulatory clause to
                  the control that satisfies it.

  Technique       Build and maintain a living traceability table linking
  Details         every regulatory clause to its governing control and
                  control owner.

  Recommended     LibreOffice Calc
  Open-Source

Task 3 — [PROJECT] Sequence control design against the fixed deadline

Step 1 — Sequence the design against the deadline

Sequencing backward from a fixed, non-negotiable date, rather than
forward from today's start, surfaces resourcing gaps early enough to
escalate them rather than discovering a shortfall in the final weeks.

SIPOC for this step.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs           Process           Outputs           Customers
  -------------- ---------------- ----------------- ----------------- ----------------
  Legal /        Ranked,          Deadline-driven   Deadline-driven   Implementation
  Compliance     deadline-aware   sequencing        sequencing plan   team (next
  function;      gap register;                      with flagged      phase); Legal /
  Functional     regulatory                         risks             Compliance;
  Process        requirement                                          Change Manager.
  Owners; IT /   analysis;                                            
  Technical      existing control                                     
  Lead.          architecture.                                        

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Deadline-driven sequencing

  Technique Goal  Sequence remaining design and implementation work
                  backward from the fixed regulatory deadline.

  Technique       Build a deadline-driven schedule working backward from
  Details         the enforceable date, flagging any task whose earliest
                  feasible start already threatens the deadline.

  Recommended     OpenProject (Gantt)
  Open-Source     
  Tool

Task 4 — [CHANGE] Launch compliance-phase communications

Step 1 — Communicate the compliance requirement

Compliance communications should be honest that this change is not
optional — attempting to soften a mandatory requirement into something
that sounds discretionary usually backfires once people realize it is
not.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Communications planning

  Technique Goal  Explain the requirement and the fixed deadline clearly
                  and without minimizing its mandatory nature.

  Technique       Build a communications calendar that states the
  Details         requirement, the deadline, and the consequence of
                  non-compliance plainly.

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 5.

Step 1 — Recruit and brief champions

Compliance champions play a slightly different role than in a
discretionary change program: their focus is less on building enthusiasm
and more on catching a colleague quietly skipping a mandatory step
before it becomes a pattern.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Nomination survey & champion kickoff workshop

  Technique Goal  Stand up a peer network that can reinforce correct
                  control practice day to day.

  Technique       Nominate 1 champion per 15–20 employees, then brief
  Details         them on the specific controls and their role in
                  reinforcing correct practice.

Task 5 — [CHANGE] Log barrier reasons where Knowledge or Ability gaps

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 6.

Step 1 — Log the barrier reasons

Because Desire is largely assumed in a mandatory compliance context, the
barrier reasons that matter most here are Knowledge and Ability gaps —
people who want to comply but genuinely don't yet understand how.

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Barrier-reason logging

  Technique Goal  Capture the specific, named reason behind any Knowledge
                  or Ability score ≤ 2.

  Technique       For every cohort scoring Knowledge or Ability at 2 or
  Details         below, require a one-sentence, named barrier reason
                  before the score is accepted.

  Recommended     OpenProject (custom field)

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Test each control against the regulatory requirement

Step 1 — Test each control

Testing has to be independent of the team that implemented the control —
a self-tested control carries far less weight, both internally and with
a regulator, than one verified by someone with no stake in a passing
result.

SIPOC for this step.

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs              Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- ------------------- -------------- -------------- --------------
  Internal audit Implemented         Independent    Control test   Legal /
  function or    controls;           control        results        Compliance;
  independent    attestation         testing                       Steering
  tester;        records;                                          Committee;
  Functional     clause-to-control                                 Change
  Process        traceability map.                                 Manager.
  Owners; Legal                                                    
  / Compliance.                                                    

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Independent control testing

  Technique Goal  Test whether each control genuinely satisfies its
                  mapped regulatory clause under real conditions.

  Technique       Have internal audit or an independent tester execute
  Details         the control against real or realistic transactions,
                  comparing the result against the requirement.

  Recommended     Kiwi TCMS (adapted as a control-test tracker)
  Open-Source     
  Tool            
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Task 2 — [PROJECT] Log and remediate every test finding

Step 1 — Remediate test findings

Given the fixed deadline, remediation prioritization has to weigh
regulatory risk far above convenience — a finding tied directly to a
core regulatory clause cannot be deferred simply because it's
technically difficult.

SIPOC for this step.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs              Process        Outputs             Customers
  -------------- ------------------- -------------- ------------------- --------------
  Internal audit Implemented         Findings       Closed findings     Legal /
  function or    controls;           remediation    register with       Compliance;
  independent    attestation         sprint         corrective-action   Steering
  tester;        records;                           evidence            Committee;
  Functional     clause-to-control                                      Change
  Process        traceability map.                                      Manager.
  Owners; Legal                                                         
  / Compliance.                                                         

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Findings remediation sprint

  Technique Goal  Close every control-test finding with documented
                  corrective action before the deadline.

  Technique       Assign an owner and documented corrective action to
  Details         every finding, prioritized by regulatory risk, with
                  evidence of closure recorded.

  Recommended     Mantis BT (adapted for compliance findings)
  Open-Source

Task 3 — [CHANGE] Cross-check Knowledge / Ability against Bridges

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 7.

Step 1 — Run the Divergence Pattern check

In a compliance program this pattern often reflects resentment at a
mandatory change rather than genuine loss — worth distinguishing, since
the response (and the risk it represents) differs.

                  for the enforceable date.

Task 1 — [PROJECT] Bring controls live at the enforceable date

Step 1 — Bring controls live

There is no flexibility to slip this date the way a purely internal
go-live sometimes can — the runbook discipline here should assume zero
tolerance for delay.

SIPOC for this step.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Suppliers      Inputs         Process        Outputs        Customers
  -------------- -------------- -------------- -------------- --------------
  IT / Technical Go/no-go       Runbook        Completed,     All end users;
  Lead; Program  decision;      execution      timestamped    regulator or
  Manager;       compiled                      go-live        contract
  Executive      evidence                                     counterparty
  Sponsor.       package; the                                 (as
                 enforceable                                  applicable);
                 regulatory                                   Steering
                 date.                                        Committee.

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------

Technique detail for this step.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Element         Detail
  --------------- -------------------------------------------------------
  Technique Name  Runbook execution

  Technique Goal  Activate every control precisely at or before the
                  enforceable date.

  Technique       Execute the go-live runbook task by task, with a second
  Details         person confirming completion of each step before the
                  enforceable date.

  Recommended     Rundeck
  Open-Source     
  Tool

This announcement should reiterate, plainly, that the control is now
mandatory as of this date — clarity here reduces the chance of an early,
avoidable non-compliance incident from someone assuming a grace period
exists.

journi: This is entered/updated in journi — Module 7.

Step 1 — Re-pulse the population post-go-live

A Kübler-Ross reading stuck at Resistance/Anger this far past go-live is
worth investigating, even in a mandatory-compliance context, since
sustained resentment increases the risk of a control being quietly
circumvented.

Task 4 — [CHANGE] Coach any cohort showing regression to old practice

Step 1 — Coach the regressing cohort

A regression here needs to be treated with more urgency than a similar
regression in a discretionary change type, precisely because a control
lapse carries direct compliance risk.

Task 1 — [JOINT] Embed compliance metrics into standard risk reporting

Step 1 — Embed metrics into risk reporting

A compliance metric visible only on a closing project's dashboard
disappears exactly when ongoing regulatory scrutiny is most likely to
ask for it; embedding it into standard risk reporting is what keeps it
available.

Failure Modes to Watch For — Compliance-Driven Change

-   Training completion mistaken for compliance — a 100%
    training-completion rate is evidence of Awareness and Knowledge, not
    proof the control is actually being followed in daily operation; the
    monitoring cycle in P6 has to test practice, not attendance records.

-   Deadline-driven scope-cutting — because the end date is fixed and
    non-negotiable, the temptation under time pressure is to narrow the
    control scope quietly rather than escalate a genuine resourcing gap;
    any scope reduction against the original gap analysis should be an
    explicit, documented, Sponsor-approved decision, not a silent one.

7. Governance & Tracking

The governance cadence below is common across all six types; the only
adjustment by type is who sits in the room. IMS programs should add the
internal audit lead from Design onward; Compliance-Driven Change
programs should add Legal/Compliance as a standing Steering Committee
member for the program's duration.

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Forum          Frequency      Attendees         Purpose
  -------------- -------------- ----------------- ----------------------------
  CM Standup     Weekly         CM, champions     Review barrier-reason notes
                                                  and the weekly dashboard.

  Steering       Monthly        ES, PM, CM, FPOs  Review Composite Readiness
  Committee                                       Index trend and phase-call
  Readout                                         justification.

  Phase Gate     End of each    ES, PM, CM        Confirm the phase's
  Review         phase                            framework reading against
                                                  evidence before the next
                                                  phase opens.

  Validation     End of Phase 4 ES, PM, CM, FPOs  Confirm findings are closed
  Sign-Off       (Validation)                     and the type's specific
  (Pilot / Mock                                   validation gate is passed.
  Audit / UAT)                                    

  Deployment     Immediately    ES, PM, CM, FPOs  Confirm readiness against
  Go/No-Go       before Phase 5                   benchmarking bands before
                 (Deployment)                     the deployment event.
  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

types.
